Anything not\ncovered here is an implementation choice.\n\n## `src/auth.ts`\n\n### `startOAuthCallbackServer(port)`\n\nReturns **synchronously** (not a `Promise`) an object of this shape:\n\n```ts\ninterface OAuthCallbackServer {\n  readonly port: number;   // may change from the requested port (see fallback below)\n  ready: Promise<void>;    // resolves once the socket is bound and accepting requests\n  token: Promise<string>;  // resolves with the raw token string, or rejects on error/cancel\n  close(): void;\n}\n```\n\nCallers do `const callback = startOAuthCallbackServer(0); await callback.ready;`\n— i.e. the handle itself is available before the socket is guaranteed to be\nbound; only `ready` tells you binding finished (and, on a fallback, that\n`port` has its final value).\n\nBehavior:\n\n- Binds to `127.0.0.1` on `port`. If that port is already taken, retries once\n  on an OS-assigned free port (`port` reflects the port actually bound).\n- Serves `GET /auth/ping` → `200 ok`.\n- Serves `GET /auth/callback`, reading `token`, `state`, `error`, and\n  `error_description` from the query string with a parser that treats a\n  literal `?` the same as `&` (so `?state=<nonce>?token=<value>` yields\n  `token=<value>`, matching the redirect quirk some hosted login pages have).\n- `token` resolves with the **raw, unverified** token string exactly as\n  received in the query. This function does not call the auth backend and\n  does not check `state` against anything: unlike the desktop overlay's\n  equivalent Rust listener (`tauri/src/main.rs`, `handle_callback_connection`),\n  which is handed an `expected_state` nonce it generated itself,\n  `startOAuthCallbackServer` takes only a `port` — it has no nonce to compare\n  against, so any `state` value is accepted and ignored at this layer.\n  Verifying the token against the auth backend (if a caller wants that) is a\n  separate, higher-level concern layered on top of the raw listener, not part\n  of this function.\n- `?error=access_denied` → `400`, body contains \"cancelled\" (case-insensitive),\n  and `token` rejects with an `Error` matching `/cancelled/i`.\n  Any other `?error=...` → `400`, using `error_description` (HTML-escaped in\n  the body) when present, and `token` rejects with an `Error` whose message\n  contains the raw, un-escaped description.\n- Every `/auth/callback` response sets `Cache-Control: no-store`.\n\n## `cloudflare/auth-worker/src/index.ts`\n\n### `GET /cli/handoff`\n\n- Validate `next` first: it must parse as a URL with `protocol === \"http:\"`\n  and `hostname` of `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`. Reject anything else with\n  `400` before touching the session.\n- Decide \"signed in\" from `auth.api.getSession({ headers: request.headers })`\n  — treat the browser as signed in when the result has a `user`. Read the\n  literal token value to attach to `next` from the request's `Cookie` header,\n  specifically the `better-auth.session_token` cookie (better-auth's default\n  cookie name) — not from a field on the object `getSession` returns, since\n  that object's shape (e.g. whether it nests a raw `token`) is not something\n  callers should depend on here.\n- No session or no cookie → `401`, with a body that (case-insensitively)\n  contains \"oauth\" (e.g. point at `gamepigeon auth oauth google` / `github` as\n  a next step).\n- On success, append `token=<cookie token>` to `next`'s existing query\n  (preserving anything already there, e.g. `state`) and respond `200` with an\n  HTML page that redirects the browser to that URL. The response body must\n  contain the literal, un-escaped resulting URL somewhere in it — e.g. inside\n  an inline `<script>` performing the redirect (`location.replace(...)`), in\n  addition to any HTML-escaped `<meta http-equiv=\"refresh\">` copy kept for\n  browsers with scripting disabled.\n\n### `GET /cli/oauth`\n\n- Reject any `provider` other than `google`/`github` with `400`.\n- If the requested provider has no client id/secret configured in the\n  environment, respond `503`.\n- Validate `next` the same way `/cli/handoff` does, before starting sign-in.\n\n","numLines":86,"startLine":1,"totalLines":86}}}

Every opener returns promptly (it hands the URL to the browser and\n/// exits), so this doesn't block the sign-in click for any noticeable time.\nfn run_opener(command: &str, args: &[&str]) -> bool {\n    Command::new(command)\n        .args(args)\n        .status()\n        .map(|status| status.success())\n        .unwrap_or(false)\n}\n\nfn applescript_escape(value: &str) -> String {\n    value.replace('\\\\', \"\\\\\\\\\").replace('\"', \"\\\\\\\"\")\n}\n\n/// The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n/// Used only to detect Arc (see `open_in_arc`); Failed(String),\n}\n\n// Tolerant of both `&` and `?` separators: some hosted redirects append `?token`\n// onto a callback that already carried `?state`, yielding `state=..?token=..`.\nfn mid-character in malformed input.\n    fn hex(byte: u8) -> Option<u8> {\n        match byte {\n            b'0'..=b'9' => Some(byte - b'0'),\n            b'a'..=b'f' => Some(byte - b'a' + 10),\n            b'A'..=b'F' => Some(byte - b'A' + 10),\n            _ => None,\n        }\n    }\n    let bytes = value.as_bytes();\n    let mut out: Vec<u8> = Vec::with_capacity(bytes.len());\n    let mut index Approve the consent screen rather than closing it.\",\n        ],\n        \"Prefer the terminal? <code>gamepigeon auth login</code> signs in the same account.\",\n    )\n}\n\n/// A redirect arrived for a sign-in attempt the overlay is no longer waiting on\n/// — usually a stale tab from a cancelled or superseded attempt.\npub fn stale_attempt_page() -> String {\n    arcade_page(\n        \"warn\",\n        \"This sign-in link is out of date\",\n        \"Tui is waiting on a newer sign-in, so this tab can't be used to finish it.\",\n        &[\n            \"Close this tab and finish in the most recent Tui sign-in tab.\",\n            \"No other tab open? Go back to Tui and choose <b>Sign in</b> again.\",\n        ],\n        \"\",\n    )\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn escapes_html_in_page_titles_and_text() {\n        assert_eq!(escape_html(\"a<b>&\\\"'\"), \"a&lt;b&gt;&amp;&quot;&#39;\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn success_page_is_a_branded_standalone_document() {\n        let page = signed_in_page();\n        assert!(page.starts_with(\"<!doctype html>\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"You&#39;re signed in\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"close this tab\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"Tui\"));\n        // Self-contained: nothing to fetch from the network.\n        assert!(!page.contains(\"http://\"));\n        assert!(!page.contains(\"https://\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn failure_pages_say_what_to_do_next() {\n        let failed = sign_in_failed_page(\"The login page came back without a session token.\");\n        assert!(failed.contains(\"without a session token\"));\n        assert!(failed.contains(\"Sign in</b> again\"));\n\n        let stale = stale_attempt_page();\n        assert!(stale.contains(\"out of date\"));\n        assert!(stale.contains(\"Sign in</b> again\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn failure_reason_is_escaped_by_the_caller_not_injected_raw() {\n        // Callers escape request-derived text; the page must carry it verbatim\n        // so already-escaped entities are not double-escaped.\n        let page = sign_in_failed_page(&escape_html(\"<script>alert(1)</script>\"));\n        assert!(!page.contains(\"<script>\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"&lt;script&gt;\"));\n    }\n}\n","numLines":184,"startLine":1,"totalLines":184}}}

The smoke command runs its TypeScript entrypoint\nwith Node's `--experimental-strip-types` support and does not add a transpiler\ndependency.\n\n`npm run dev` runs `wrangler dev --env dev`. Wrangler keeps the development D1\ndatabase and Durable Object SQLite data in its local state directory. Cloudflare requires migration history to stay append-only, so those\nentries remain even though no code references `GameRoom` anymore.\n\nThe repository keeps `wrangler.toml`: Wrangler supports both TOML and JSONC,\nand changing formats would not change the deployment. The top-level\nconfiguration is production. `env.dev` is local-only and is named\n`tui-gamepigeon-auth-dev` so it cannot be confused with production. There is no\n`env.staging` until an operator provisions a separate staging Worker and D1\ndatabase; never point a future staging binding at the production database ID.\n\n### Public URLs and origins\n\nSet these non-secret variables in the relevant Wrangler environment:\n\n- `BETTER_AUTH_URL`: absolute public Worker URL, with no trailing slash.\n- `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`: comma-separated origins Better Auth may trust\n  for cross-origin auth requests. Keep the CLI callback origin and Worker origin.\n- `PUBLIC_GAME_URL`: absolute HTTPS URL for the app.\n\nExample production values:\n\n```toml\n[vars]\nBETTER_AUTH_URL = \"https://auth.runretroarcade.com\"\nBETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS = \"http://127.0.0.1:3210,https://auth.runretroarcade.com,https://runretroarcade.com\"\nPUBLIC_GAME_URL = \"https://runretroarcade.com\"\n```\n\n### Secrets\n\n`BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` and the separate operator-only `MIGRATION_SECRET` are\nrequired. Do not commit this file.\n\n```dotenv\nBETTER_AUTH_SECRET=replace-with-a-long-random-development-secret\nMIGRATION_SECRET=replace-with-a-different-long-random-development-secret\n```\n\nWith the local Worker running, initialize the Better Auth tables once:\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer replace-with-a-different-long-random-development-secret\" \\\n  http://localhost:8787/migrate\n```\n\nPoint a local client at the Worker with\n`GAMEPIGEON_AUTH_URL=http://localhost:8787` and\n`GAMEPIGEON_LOGIN_URL=http://localhost:5173/login.html`.\n\n## Cloudflare configuration\n\n### D1\n\nCreate the production D1 database and place its ID in the top-level\n`database_id` in `wrangler.toml`:\n\n```bash\nnpx wrangler login\nnpx wrangler d1 create tui-gamepigeon-auth\n```\n\nThe `DB` binding stores Better Auth data, initialized by calling\n`POST /migrate` with the operator bearer secret after an approved deployment.\n\n`wrangler.toml` still lists a retired `GameRoom` Durable Object migration\n(`v1` create, `v2` delete) from a multiplayer feature that has since been\nremoved. Cloudflare requires migration history to stay append-only, so those\nentries remain even though no code references `GameRoom` anymore.\n\nThe repository keeps `wrangler.toml`: Wrangler supports both TOML and JSONC,\nand changing formats would not change the deployment. The top-level\nconfiguration is production. `env.dev` is local-only and is named\n`tui-gamepigeon-auth-dev` so it cannot be confused with production. There is no\n`env.staging` until an operator provisions a separate staging Worker and D1\ndatabase; never point a future staging binding at the production database ID.\n\n### Public URLs and origins\n\nSet these non-secret variables in the relevant Wrangler environment:\n\n- `BETTER_AUTH_URL`: absolute public Worker URL, with no trailing slash.\n- `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`: comma-separated origins Better Auth may trust\n  for cross-origin auth requests. Keep the CLI callback origin and Worker origin.\n- `PUBLIC_GAME_URL`: absolute HTTPS URL for the app.\n\nExample production values:\n\n```toml\n[vars]\nBETTER_AUTH_URL = \"https://auth.runretroarcade.com\"\nBETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS = \"http://127.0.0.1:3210,https://auth.runretroarcade.com,https://runretroarcade.com\"\nPUBLIC_GAME_URL = \"https://runretroarcade.com\"\n```\n\n### Secrets\n\n`BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` and the separate operator-only `MIGRATION_SECRET` are\nrequired. Kept as a\n * closed list rather than passing the query parameter through to Better Auth,\n * so an unconfigured or mistyped provider fails here with a clear status\n * instead of somewhere inside the OAuth redirect chain.\n */\nexport const CLI_OAUTH_PROVIDERS = [\"google\", \"github\"] as const;\n\nexport type CliOAuthProvider = (typeof CLI_OAUTH_PROVIDERS)[number];\n\nexport function isCliOAuthProvider(value: string | null): value is CliOAuthProvider {\n  return CLI_OAUTH_PROVIDERS.includes(value as CliOAuthProvider);\n}\n\ninterface ProviderCredentials {\n  clientId: string;\n  clientSecret: string;\n}\n\nfunction providerCredentials(env: AuthEnv, provider: CliOAuthProvider): ProviderCredentials | null {\n  const clientId = (provider === \"google\" ? env.GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID : env.GITHUB_CLIENT_ID)?.trim();\n  const clientSecret = (provider === \"google\" ? env.GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET : env.GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET)\n    ?.trim();\n  if (!clientId || !clientSecret) return null;\n  return { clientId, clientSecret };\n}\n\n/**\n * Whether this deployment has both halves of a provider's credentials. A\n * Worker deployed without them still serves email/password sign-in, so\n * `/cli/oauth` reports the gap (503) rather than pretending the provider is a\n * user error.\n */\nexport function oauthProviderConfigured(env: AuthEnv, provider: CliOAuthProvider): boolean {\n  return providerCredentials(env, provider) !== null;\n}\n\nfunction socialProviders(env: AuthEnv): Record<string, ProviderCredentials> {\n  const configured: Record<string, ProviderCredentials> = {};\n  for (const provider of CLI_OAUTH_PROVIDERS) {\n    const credentials = providerCredentials(env, provider);\n    if (credentials) configured[provider] = credentials;\n  }\n  return configured;\n}\n\n/**\n * Loopback on *any* port: the CLI listener asks for 3210 but falls back to an\n * OS-assigned port when something else already holds it (see\n * `startOAuthCallbackServer` in `src/auth.ts`), so the port a callback URL will\n * carry is not knowable when this list is built. `/cli/oauth` and\n * `/cli/handoff` narrow this to the same scheme/host pair themselves before\n * ever attaching a token, so widening Better Auth's list here does not widen\n * where a session can be handed to.\n *\n * Deliberately not part of `trustedOrigins()`: that list is also the exact\n * allow-list for CORS, which must stay a literal origin match.\n */\nconst LOOPBACK_CALLBACK_PATTERNS = [\"http://127.0.0.1:*\", \"http://localhost:*\"];\n\nexport function trustedOrigins(env: AuthEnv): string[] {\n  const configured = (env.BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS ?? * spans; the title is escaped. Escape request-derived text at the call site.\n */\nfunction arcadePage({ tone, title, lead, steps = [], hint = \"\", head = \"\", script = \"\" }: ArcadePage): string {\n  const list = steps.length === 0\n    ? \"\"\n    : `<ul class=\"steps\">${steps.map((step) => `<li>${step}</li>`).join(\"\")}</ul>`;\n  const note = hint ? `<p class=\"hint\">${hint}</p>` : \"\";\n  const inline = script ? `<script>${script}</script>` : \"\";\n  const icon = tone === \"ok\" ? Callers pass a serialized `URL`, whose percent-encoding already rules\n * out the `<` that a `</script>` break-out would need.\n */\nexport function handoffRedirectPage(target: string): string {\n  return arcadePage({\n    tone: \"ok\",\n    title: \"Signing you in\",\n    lead: \"Handing your session back to the terminal. Both CLI routes hand a browser\n * session to whatever this returns, so it is deliberately narrow: plain `http`\n * on loopback, which is the only thing a locally running CLI listener can be.\n * Any port is fine (the listener falls back off 3210 when it is busy), but an\n * `https` host, a non-loopback host, or a non-URL is refused before either\n * route touches the session.\n */\nfunction cliNextTarget(raw: string | null): URL | null {\n  if (!raw) return null;\n  let target: URL;\n  try {\n    target = new URL(raw);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n  if (target.protocol !== \"http:\") return null;\n  if (target.hostname !== \"127.0.0.1\" && target.hostname !== \"localhost\") return null;\n  return target;\n}\n\n/**\n * The literal session token from the request's `Cookie` header.\n *\n * Read from the cookie rather than from whatever `getSession()` returns,\n * because that object's shape (in particular whether it nests a raw `token`)\n * is a Better Auth implementation detail this handoff should not depend on.\n * The value is passed through byte for byte: the `bearer` plugin verifies the\n * `<token>.<signature>` pair the cookie holds, so re-encoding it would break\n * the signature. Values outside the character set a signed cookie can produce\n * are treated as absent — they cannot be a real token, and refusing them keeps\n * the un-escaped copy in `handoffRedirectPage` inert.\n */\nfunction sessionCookieToken(request: Request): string | null {\n  const header = request.headers.get(\"cookie\");\n  if (!header) return null;\n  for (const part of header.split(\";\")) {\n    const separator = part.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator === -1) continue;\n    const name = part.slice(0, separator).trim();\n    // An `https` deployment gets the `__Secure-` prefixed variant of the same\n    // cookie; `http://localhost:8787` in dev gets the bare name.\n    if (name !== SESSION_COOKIE && name !== `__Secure-${SESSION_COOKIE}`) continue;\n    const value = part.slice(separator + 1).trim();\n    if (value && /^[A-Za-z0-9._~%+/=-]+$/.test(value)) return value;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Hands the browser's session back to a waiting CLI listener. The CLI opened\n * `/cli/oauth`, the provider sent the browser back here with a session cookie,\n * and this is the last hop: `next` plus a `token` query parameter.\n */\nasync function cliHandoff(request: Request, url: URL, env: AuthEnv): Promise<Response> {\n  const next = cliNextTarget(url.searchParams.get(\"next\"));\n  if (!next) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      400,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\"This sign-in link points somewhere Tui won't hand a session to.\"),\n    );\n  }\n\n  const session = await createAuth(env).api.getSession({ headers: request.headers });\n  const token = session?.user ? sessionCookieToken(request) : null;\n  if (!token) return htmlResponse(401, signInRequiredPage());\n\n  // Appended by hand rather than through `searchParams`, so the token lands\n  // verbatim and anything `next` already carries (a `state` nonce, say) keeps\n  // its original spelling.\n  const target = `${next.toString()}${next.search ? \"&\" : \"?\"}token=${token}`;\n  return htmlResponse(200, handoffRedirectPage(target));\n}\n\n/**\n * Starts a browser sign-in for the CLI: validate what we were asked for, then\n * redirect to the provider with `/cli/handoff` queued up as the return trip.\n */\nasync function cliOAuth(request: Request, url: URL, env: AuthEnv): Promise<Response> {\n  const provider = url.searchParams.get(\"provider\");\n  if (!isCliOAuthProvider(provider)) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      400,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\"Tui can only sign in with Google or GitHub in the browser.\"),\n    );\n  }\n  if (!oauthProviderConfigured(env, provider)) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      503,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\n        `This Tui server has no ${provider === \"google\" ? \"Google\" : \"GitHub\"} credentials configured yet.`,\n      ),\n    );\n  }\n  const next = cliNextTarget(url.searchParams.get(\"next\"));\n  if (!next) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      400,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\"This sign-in link points somewhere Tui won't hand a session to.\"),\n    );\n  }\n\n  const handoff = new URL(\"/cli/handoff\", url.origin);\n  handoff.searchParams.set(\"next\", next.toString());\n  let authorizeUrl: string | undefined;\n  let cookies: string[] = [];\n  try {\n    // `disableRedirect` keeps Better Auth from deciding the response for us:\n    // we need its `Set-Cookie` (the OAuth state, which the callback verifies)\n    // on a redirect we build ourselves. A provider failure comes back through\n    // `errorCallbackURL`, i.e. straight to the CLI listener, which turns\n    // `?error=...` into a readable reason instead of a silent hang.\n    const started = await createAuth(env).api.signInSocial({\n      body: {\n        provider,\n        callbackURL: handoff.toString(),\n        errorCallbackURL: next.toString(),\n        disableRedirect: true,\n      },\n      headers: request.headers,\n      asResponse: true,\n    });\n    if (started.ok) {\n      authorizeUrl = ((await started.json()) as { url?: string }).url;\n      cookies = started.headers.getSetCookie();\n    }\n  } catch {\n    authorizeUrl = undefined;\n  }\n  if (!authorizeUrl) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      502,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\n        `Tui couldn't reach ${provider === \"google\" ? \"Google\" : \"GitHub\"} to start the sign-in.`,\n      ),\n    );\n  }\n\n  const headers = new Headers({\n    location: authorizeUrl,\n    \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n    \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n  });\n  for (const cookie of cookies) headers.append(\"set-cookie\", cookie);\n  return new Response(null { status: 302, headers });\n}\n\nfunction authCors(request: Request, response: Response, env: AuthEnv): Response {"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":386,"cache_read_input_tokens":88612,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":386,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9663849a-76cb-49a5-bc3c-bcfc035a1e42","uuid":"b1e82419-a8fb-40a3-8388-f26d71d841c5","timestamp":"2026-09-09T13:05:44.468Z"}

Both CLI routes hand a browser\n * session to whatever this returns, so it is deliberately narrow: plain `http`\n * on loopback, which is the only thing a locally running CLI listener can be.\n * Any port is fine (the listener falls back off 3210 when it is busy), but an\n * `https` host, a non-loopback host, or a non-URL is refused before either\n * route touches the session.\n */\nfunction cliNextTarget(raw: string | null): URL | null {\n  if (!raw) return null;\n  let target: URL;\n  try {\n    target = new URL(raw);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n  if (target.protocol !== \"http:\") return null;\n  if (target.hostname !== \"127.0.0.1\" && target.hostname !== \"localhost\") return null;\n  return target;\n}\n\n/**\n * The literal session token from the request's `Cookie` header.\n *\n * Read from the cookie rather than from whatever `getSession()` returns,\n * because that object's shape (in particular whether it nests a raw `token`)\n * is a Better Auth implementation detail this handoff should not depend on.\n * The value is passed through byte for byte: the `bearer` plugin verifies the\n * `<token>.<signature>` pair the cookie holds, so re-encoding it would break\n * the signature. Values outside the character set a signed cookie can produce\n * are treated as absent — they cannot be a real token, and refusing them keeps\n * the un-escaped copy in `handoffRedirectPage` inert.\n */\nfunction sessionCookieToken(request: Request): string | null {\n  const header = request.headers.get(\"cookie\");\n  if (!header) return null;\n  for (const part of header.split(\";\")) {\n    const separator = part.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator === -1) continue;\n    const name = part.slice(0, separator).trim();\n    // An `https` deployment gets the `__Secure-` prefixed variant of the same\n    // cookie; `http://localhost:8787` in dev gets the bare name.\n    if (name !== SESSION_COOKIE && name !== `__Secure-${SESSION_COOKIE}`) continue;\n    const value = part.slice(separator + 1).trim();\n    if (value && /^[A-Za-z0-9._~%+/=-]+$/.test(value)) return value;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Hands the browser's session back to a waiting CLI listener. The CLI opened\n * `/cli/oauth`, the provider sent the browser back here with a session cookie,\n * and this is the last hop: `next` plus a `token` query parameter.\n */\nasync function cliHandoff(request: Request, url: URL, env: AuthEnv): Promise<Response> {\n  const next = cliNextTarget(url.searchParams.get(\"next\"));\n  if (!next) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      400,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\"This sign-in link points somewhere Tui won't hand a session to.\"),\n    );\n  }\n\n  const session = await createAuth(env).api.getSession({ headers: request.headers });\n  const token = session?.user ? sessionCookieToken(request) : null;\n  if (!token) return htmlResponse(401, signInRequiredPage());\n\n  // Appended by hand rather than through `searchParams`, so the token lands\n  // verbatim and anything `next` already carries (a `state` nonce, say) keeps\n  // its original spelling.\n  const target = `${next.toString()}${next.search ? \"&\" : \"?\"}token=${token}`;\n  return htmlResponse(200, handoffRedirectPage(target));\n}\n\n/**\n * Starts a browser sign-in for the CLI: validate what we were asked for, then\n * redirect to the provider with `/cli/handoff` queued up as the return trip.\n */\nasync function cliOAuth(request: Request, url: URL, env: AuthEnv): Promise<Response> {\n  const provider = url.searchParams.get(\"provider\");\n  if (!isCliOAuthProvider(provider)) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      400,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\"Tui can only sign in with Google or GitHub in the browser.\"),\n    );\n  }\n  if (!oauthProviderConfigured(env, provider)) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      503,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\n        `This Tui server has no ${provider === \"google\" ? \"Google\" : \"GitHub\"} credentials configured yet.`,\n      ),\n    );\n  }\n  const next = cliNextTarget(url.searchParams.get(\"next\"));\n  if (!next) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      400,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\"This sign-in link points somewhere Tui won't hand a session to.\"),\n    );\n  }\n\n  const handoff = new URL(\"/cli/handoff\", url.origin);\n  handoff.searchParams.set(\"next\", next.toString());\n  let authorizeUrl: string | undefined;\n  let cookies: string[] = [];\n  try {\n    // `disableRedirect` keeps Better Auth from deciding the response for us:\n    // we need its `Set-Cookie` (the OAuth state, which the callback verifies)\n    // on a redirect we build ourselves. A provider failure comes back through\n    // `errorCallbackURL`, i.e. straight to the CLI listener, which turns\n    // `?error=...` into a readable reason instead of a silent hang.\n    const started = await createAuth(env).api.signInSocial({\n      body: {\n        provider,\n        callbackURL: handoff.toString(),\n        errorCallbackURL: next.toString(),\n        disableRedirect: true,\n      },\n      headers: request.headers,\n      asResponse: true,\n    });\n    if (started.ok) {\n      authorizeUrl = ((await started.json()) as { url?: string }).url;\n      cookies = started.headers.getSetCookie();\n    }\n  } catch {\n    authorizeUrl = undefined;\n  }\n  if (!authorizeUrl) {\n    return htmlResponse(\n      502,\n      handoffRefusedPage(\n        `Tui couldn't reach ${provider === \"google\" ? \"Google\" : \"GitHub\"} to start the sign-in.`,\n      ),\n    );\n  }\n\n  const headers = new Headers({\n    location: authorizeUrl,\n    \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n    \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n  });\n  for (const cookie of cookies) headers.append(\"set-cookie\", cookie);\n  return new Response(null { status: 302, headers });\n}\n\nfunction authCors(request: Request, response: Response, env: AuthEnv): Response {\n  const origin = request.headers.get(\"origin\");\n  if (!origin || !trustedOrigins(env).includes(origin)) return response;\n  const headers = new Headers(response.headers);\n  headers.set(\"access-control-allow-origin\", origin);\n  headers.set(\"access-control-allow-credentials\", \"true\");\n  headers.set(\"access-control-expose-headers\", \"set-auth-token\");\n  headers.append(\"vary\", \"Origin\");\n  return new Response(response.body, { status: response.status, statusText: response.statusText, headers });\n}\n\nexport default {\n  async fetch(request: Request, env: AuthEnv): Promise<Response> {\n    const url = new URL(request.url);\n\n    if (url.pathname.startsWith(\"/api/auth/\") && request.method === \"OPTIONS\") {\n      const origin = request.headers.get(\"origin\");\n      if (!origin || !trustedOrigins(env).includes(origin)) return new Response(null, { status: 403 });\n      return authCors(request, new Response(null, {\n        status: 204,\n        headers: {\n          \"access-control-allow-headers\": \"content-type, authorization\",\n          \"access-control-allow-methods\": \"GET, POST, OPTIONS\",\n        },\n      }), env);\n    }\n\n    if (url.pathname === \"/migrate\" && request.method === \"POST\") {\n \"Unauthorized\" },\n          { status: 401, headers: { \"www-authenticate\": \"Bearer\" } },\n        );\n      }\n      const auth = createAuth(env);\n      const { toBeCreated, toBeAdded, runMigrations } = await getMigrations(auth.options);\n      if (toBeCreated.length === 0 && toBeAdded.length === 0) {\n        return Response.json({ message: \"No migrations needed\" });\n      }\n      await runMigrations();\n      return Response.json({\n        message: \"Migrations completed\",\n        created: toBeCreated.map((table) => table.table),\n        added: toBeAdded.map((table) => table.table),\n      });\n    }\n\n    if (url.pathname === \"/\" && request.method === \"GET\") {\n      return Response.json({\n        service: \"tui-gamepigeon-auth\",\n        ok: true,\n        authBasePath: \"/api/auth\",\n      });\n    }\n\n    return authCors(request, await createAuth(env).handler(request), env);\n  },\n} satisfies Some hosted\n * redirects append `?token=...` onto a callback URL that already carried\n * `?state=...`, yielding `state=<nonce>?token=<value>`; the desktop overlay's\n * Rust listener is tolerant of exactly the same quirk.\n */\nfunction callbackParams(target: string): Map<string, string> {\n  const params = new Map<string, string>();\n  const start = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  if (start === -1) return params;\n  for (const pair of target.slice(start + 1).split(/[&?]/)) {\n    if (!pair) continue;\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    const key = separator === -1 ? pair : pair.slice(0, separator);\n    const value = separator === -1 ? \"\" : pair.slice(separator + 1);\n    if (!params.has(key)) params.set(key, value);\n  }\n  return params;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim,\n * because the auth Worker signed the exact bytes it put in the URL.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replaceAll(\"+\", \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. Spawning alone\n * can't tell us that — `xdg-open` with no handler and `open` with a broken\n * default browser both fail *after* the spawn — so we wait for the exit status.\n * Every opener returns promptly (it hands the URL to the browser and exits).\n */\nfunction runOpener(command: string, args: string[]): boolean {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(command, args, { stdio: \"ignore\" });\n    return !result.error && result.status === 0;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * `defaults read` prints one dict per registered handler; the role and the\n * scheme are separate lines inside the same `{ ... }` block, so we scan block\n * by block for the one claiming `https`.\n */\nfunction parseDefaultHttpsHandler(plist: string): string | null {\n  for (const block of plist.split(\"{\")) {\n    if (!block.includes(\"LSHandlerURLScheme = https;\")) continue;\n    const line = block.split(\"\\n\").find((entry) => entry.trimStart().startsWith(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \"));\n    if (!line) return null;\n    const value = line.trim().slice(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \".length).replace(/;$/, \"\").replace(/^\"|\"$/g, \"\");\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n * Used only to detect Arc; any parse failure just means we take the ordinary\n * `open` path.\n */\nfunction defaultHttpsHandler(): string | null {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(\"defaults\", [\"read\", \"com.apple.LaunchServices/com.apple.launchservices.secure\"], {\n      encoding: \"utf8\",\n    });\n    if (result.error || !result.stdout) return null;\n    return parseDefaultHttpsHandler(result.stdout);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Hands the sign-in URL to the user's browser. Some hosted\n * redirects append `?token=...` onto a callback URL that already carried\n * `?state=...`, yielding `state=<nonce>?token=<value>`; the desktop overlay's\n * Rust listener is tolerant of exactly the same quirk.\n */\nfunction callbackParams(target: string): Map<string, string> {\n  const params = new Map<string, string>();\n  const start = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  if (start === -1) return params;\n  for (const pair of target.slice(start + 1).split(/[&?]/)) {\n    if (!pair) continue;\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    const key = separator === -1 ? pair : pair.slice(0, separator);\n    const value = separator === -1 ? \"\" : pair.slice(separator + 1);\n    if (!params.has(key)) params.set(key, value);\n  }\n  return params;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim,\n * because the auth Worker signed the exact bytes it put in the URL.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replaceAll(\"+\", \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. Capped\n * because it lands in both the browser page and the terminal.\n */\nfunction callbackFailure(params: Map<string, string>): string | null {\n  const error = params.get(\"error\");\n  if (!error) return null;\n  if (error === \"access_denied\") return \"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\";\n  const description = decodeQueryText(params.get(\"error_description\") ?? \"\");\n  const message = description || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return message.slice(0, 200);\n}\n\n/**\n * Listens on loopback for the browser to hand back a session.\n *\n * Returns synchronously so a caller can read `port` off the handle it is about\n * to await; only `ready` says the socket is bound (and, after a fallback, that\n * `port` is final).\n *\n * The token resolves **raw and unverified**. Checking the token against the auth\n * backend is a separate concern layered on top (see `runOAuthLogin`).\n */\nexport function startOAuthCallbackServer(port: number): OAuthCallbackServer {\n  let boundPort = port;\n  let settled = false;\n  let resolveToken!: (token: string) => void;\n  let rejectToken!: (error: Error) => void;\n  const token = new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveToken = resolve;\n    rejectToken = reject;\n  });\n  // A caller that closes the listener without awaiting `token` — a cancelled\n  // sign-in, or a test that only exercised /auth/ping — should not trip an\n  // unhandled rejection. The requested port is only a\n      // preference, and `port` reports what we ended up with.\n      server.once(\"error\", reject);\n      server.listen(0, loopbackHost);\n    });\n    server.listen(port, loopbackHost);\n  });\n  void ready.catch(() => {});\n\n  return {\n    get port(): number {\n      return boundPort;\n    },\n    ready,\n    token,\n    close(): void {\n      fail(\"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\");\n      // Browsers keep the callback connection alive, which would otherwise\n      // hold `close()` — and the process — open past the handoff.\n      server.closeAllConnections?.();\n      server.close();\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * Arc (and Dia, same vendor) route links from other apps into a \"Little Arc\"\n * window, which drops the auth Worker's redirect chain — the tab either never\n * appears or dies mid-handoff, so sign-in silently never completes. Asking Arc\n * for a real tab in a real window over AppleScript bypasses Little Arc\n * entirely, the same way the desktop overlay does it.\n */\nexport function isArcBundle(bundleId: string): boolean {\n  const id = bundleId.trim().toLowerCase();\n  return id === \"company.thebrowser.browser\" || id === \"company.thebrowser.dia\";\n}\n\nfunction applescriptEscape(value: string): string {\n  return value.replaceAll(\"\\\\\", \"\\\\\\\\\").replaceAll('\"', '\\\\\"');\n}\n\n/** The AppleScript that opens `url` as a real tab in a real Arc/Dia window. */\nexport function arcTabScript(bundleId: string, url: string): string {\n  return `tell application id \"${applescriptEscape(bundleId)}\"\n    activate\n    if (count of windows) is 0 then\n        make new window\n    end if\n    tell front window to make new tab with properties {URL:\"${applescriptEscape(url)}\"}\nend tell`;\n}\n\n/**\n * Runs an opener and reports whether it actually succeeded. Spawning alone\n * can't tell us that — `xdg-open` with no handler and `open` with a broken\n * default browser both fail *after* the spawn — so we wait for the exit status.\n * Every opener returns promptly (it hands the URL to the browser and exits).\n */\nfunction runOpener(command: string, args: string[]): boolean {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(command, args, { stdio: \"ignore\" });\n    return !result.error && result.status === 0;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * `defaults read` prints one dict per registered handler; the role and the\n * scheme are separate lines inside the same `{ ... }` block, so we scan block\n * by block for the one claiming `https`.\n */\nfunction parseDefaultHttpsHandler(plist: string): string | null {\n  for (const block of plist.split(\"{\")) {\n    if (!block.includes(\"LSHandlerURLScheme = https;\")) continue;\n    const line = block.split(\"\\n\").find((entry) => entry.trimStart().startsWith(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \"));\n    if (!line) return null;\n    const value = line.trim().slice(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \".length).replace(/;$/, \"\").replace(/^\"|\"$/g, \"\");\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n * Used only to detect Arc; any parse failure just means we take the ordinary\n * `open` path.\n */\nfunction defaultHttpsHandler(): string | null {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(\"defaults\", [\"read\", \"com.apple.LaunchServices/com.apple.launchservices.secure\"], {\n      encoding: \"utf8\",\n    });\n    if (result.error || !result.stdout) return null;\n    return parseDefaultHttpsHandler(result.stdout);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Hands the sign-in URL to the user's browser. Returns false when every opener\n * we know about failed — the caller then leads with the fallback link instead\n * of claiming a browser is opening.\n */\nfunction openBrowser(url: string): boolean {\n  if (process.platform === \"darwin\") {\n    const bundleId = defaultHttpsHandler();\n    if (bundleId && isArcBundle(bundleId) && runOpener(\"osascript\", [\"-e\", arcTabScript(bundleId, url)])) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    // Safari is the last resort: it is always present, so a broken or missing\n    // default handler still gets the user to the sign-in page.\n    return runOpener(\"open\", [url]) || runOpener(\"open\", [\"-a\", \"Safari\", url]);\n  }\n  if (process.platform === \"win32\") {\n    return runOpener(\"cmd\", [\"/c\", \"start\", \"\", url])\n      || runOpener(\"rundll32\", [\"url.dll,FileProtocolHandler\", url]);\n  }\n  return runOpener(\"xdg-open\", [url])\n    || runOpener(\"gio\", [\"open\", url])\n    || runOpener(\"x-www-browser\", [url]);\n}\n\nasync function runOAuthLogin(provider: OAuthProvider): Promise<AuthSuccess> {\n  const authBaseUrl = requireAuthBaseUrl();\n  const callback = startOAuthCallbackServer(defaultCallbackPort);\n  try {\n    await callback.ready;\n    // The nonce is here so the Worker's `token=` lands on a query that already\n    // has something in it — the same round trip the overlay makes. The listener\n    // has no nonce of its own to compare it against, so it ignores whatever\n    // comes back; the token is verified against the backend below instead.\n    const next = `http://${loopbackHost}:${callback.port}/auth/callback?state=${randomBytes(16).toString(\"hex\")}`;\n    const url = `${authBaseUrl}/cli/oauth?provider=${provider}&next=${encodeURIComponent(next)}`;\n\n    if (openBrowser(url)) {\n      console.log(`Opening ${providerLabel(provider)} sign-in in your browser...`);\n      console.log(`Nothing opened? Use this link instead:\\n  ${url}`);\n    } else {\n      console.log(`Could not open a browser. Some hosted\n * redirects append `?token=...` onto a callback URL that already carried\n * `?state=...`, yielding `state=<nonce>?token=<value>`; the desktop overlay's\n * Rust listener is tolerant of exactly the same quirk.\n */\nfunction callbackParams(target: string): Map<string, string> {\n  const params = new Map<string, string>();\n  const start = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  if (start === -1) return params;\n  for (const pair of target.slice(start + 1).split(/[&?]/)) {\n    if (!pair) continue;\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    const key = separator === -1 ? pair : pair.slice(0, separator);\n    const value = separator === -1 ? \"\" : pair.slice(separator + 1);\n    if (!params.has(key)) params.set(key, value);\n  }\n  return params;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim,\n * because the auth Worker signed the exact bytes it put in the URL.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replaceAll(\"+\", \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. Capped\n * because it lands in both the browser page and the terminal.\n */\nfunction callbackFailure(params: Map<string, string>): string | null {\n  const error = params.get(\"error\");\n  if (!error) return null;\n  if (error === \"access_denied\") return \"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\";\n  const description = decodeQueryText(params.get(\"error_description\") ?? \"\");\n  const message = description || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return message.slice(0, 200);\n}\n\n/**\n * Listens on loopback for the browser to hand back a session.\n *\n * Returns synchronously so a caller can read `port` off the handle it is about\n * to await; only `ready` says the socket is bound (and, after a fallback, that\n * `port` is final).\n *\n * The token resolves **raw and unverified**. Unlike the overlay's Rust listener,\n * which generates its own nonce and can therefore reject a mismatched `state`,\n * this takes only a port: it has nothing to compare `state` against, so any\n * `state` is accepted and ignored here. Checking the token against the auth\n * backend is a separate concern layered on top (see `runOAuthLogin`).\n */\nexport function startOAuthCallbackServer(port: number): OAuthCallbackServer {\n  let boundPort = port;\n  let settled = false;\n  let resolveToken!: (token: string) => void;\n  let rejectToken!: (error: Error) => void;\n  const token = new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveToken = resolve;\n    rejectToken = reject;\n  });\n  // A caller that closes the listener without awaiting `token` — a cancelled\n  // sign-in, or a test that only exercised /auth/ping — should not trip an\n  // unhandled rejection. Attaching a handler here still leaves `token`\n  // awaitable by the caller.\n  void token.catch(() => {});\n\n  const succeed = (value: string): void => {\n    if (settled) return;\n    settled = true;\n    resolveToken(value);\n  };\n  const fail = (reason: string): void => {\n    if (settled) return;\n    settled = true;\n    rejectToken(new Error(reason));\n  };\n\n  const server = createServer((request, response) => {\n    const target = request.url ?? Asking Arc\n * for a real tab in a real window over AppleScript bypasses Little Arc\n * entirely, the same way the desktop overlay does it.\n */\nexport function isArcBundle(bundleId: string): boolean {\n  const id = bundleId.trim().toLowerCase();\n  return id === \"company.thebrowser.browser\" || id === \"company.thebrowser.dia\";\n}\n\nfunction applescriptEscape(value: string): string {\n  return value.replaceAll(\"\\\\\", \"\\\\\\\\\").replaceAll('\"', '\\\\\"');\n}\n\n/** The AppleScript that opens `url` as a real tab in a real Arc/Dia window. */\nexport function arcTabScript(bundleId: string, url: string): string {\n  return `tell application id \"${applescriptEscape(bundleId)}\"\n    activate\n    if (count of windows) is 0 then\n        make new window\n    end if\n    tell front window to make new tab with properties {URL:\"${applescriptEscape(url)}\"}\nend tell`;\n}\n\n/**\n * Runs an opener and reports whether it actually succeeded. Spawning alone\n * can't tell us that — `xdg-open` with no handler and `open` with a broken\n * default browser both fail *after* the spawn — so we wait for the exit status.\n * Every opener returns promptly (it hands the URL to the browser and exits).\n */\nfunction runOpener(command: string, args: string[]): boolean {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(command, args, { stdio: \"ignore\" });\n    return !result.error && result.status === 0;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * `defaults read` prints one dict per registered handler; the role and the\n * scheme are separate lines inside the same `{ ... }` block, so we scan block\n * by block for the one claiming `https`.\n */\nfunction parseDefaultHttpsHandler(plist: string): string | null {\n  for (const block of plist.split(\"{\")) {\n    if (!block.includes(\"LSHandlerURLScheme = https;\")) continue;\n    const line = block.split(\"\\n\").find((entry) => entry.trimStart().startsWith(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \"));\n    if (!line) return null;\n    const value = line.trim().slice(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \".length).replace(/;$/, \"\").replace(/^\"|\"$/g, \"\");\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n * Used only to detect Arc; any parse failure just means we take the ordinary\n * `open` path.\n */\nfunction defaultHttpsHandler(): string | null {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(\"defaults\", [\"read\", \"com.apple.LaunchServices/com.apple.launchservices.secure\"], {\n      encoding: \"utf8\",\n    });\n    if (result.error || !result.stdout) return null;\n    return parseDefaultHttpsHandler(result.stdout);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Hands the sign-in URL to the user's browser. The listener\n    // has no nonce of its own to compare it against, so it ignores whatever\n    // comes back; the token is verified against the backend below instead.\n    const next = `http://${loopbackHost}:${callback.port}/auth/callback?state=${randomBytes(16).toString(\"hex\")}`;\n    const url = `${authBaseUrl}/cli/oauth?provider=${provider}&next=${encodeURIComponent(next)}`;\n\n    if (openBrowser(url)) {\n      console.log(`Opening ${providerLabel(provider)} sign-in in your browser...`);\n      console.log(`Nothing opened? Use this link instead:\\n  ${url}`);\n    } else {\n      console.log(`Could not open a browser. Open this link to sign in:\\n  ${url}`);\n    }\n    console.log(`Waiting for the browser to come back on port ${callback.port}...`);\n\n    const sessionToken = await callback.token;\n    const user = await resolveUser(sessionToken);\n    saveSession(sessionToken, user);\n    console.log(`Signed in as ${user.email}`);\n    return { kind: \"signed_in\", method: provider, userId: user.id };\n  } finally {\n    callback.close();\n  }\n}\n\nexport type { AuthUser, StoredSession };\n\nexport function authConfigured(): boolean {\n  return Boolean(configuredAuthBaseUrl());\n}\n\nexport function storedSession(): StoredSession | null {\n  return readSession();\n}\n\nexport function storedUserId(): string | null {\n  return readSession()?.user.id ?? null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Validates a persisted session against the backend, dropping it if it no\n * longer resolves. Some hosted\n * redirects append `?token=...` onto a callback URL that already carried\n * `?state=...`, yielding `state=<nonce>?token=<value>`; the desktop overlay's\n * Rust listener is tolerant of exactly the same quirk.\n */\nfunction callbackParams(target: string): Map<string, string> {\n  const params = new Map<string, string>();\n  const start = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  if (start === -1) return params;\n  for (const pair of target.slice(start + 1).split(/[&?]/)) {\n    if (!pair) continue;\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    const key = separator === -1 ? pair : pair.slice(0, separator);\n    const value = separator === -1 ? \"\" : pair.slice(separator + 1);\n    if (!params.has(key)) params.set(key, value);\n  }\n  return params;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim,\n * because the auth Worker signed the exact bytes it put in the URL.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replaceAll(\"+\", \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. Capped\n * because it lands in both the browser page and the terminal.\n */\nfunction callbackFailure(params: Map<string, string>): string | null {\n  const error = params.get(\"error\");\n  if (!error) return null;\n  if (error === \"access_denied\") return \"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\";\n  const description = decodeQueryText(params.get(\"error_description\") ?? \"\");\n  const message = description || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return message.slice(0, 200);\n}\n\n/**\n * Listens on loopback for the browser to hand back a session.\n *\n * Returns synchronously so a caller can read `port` off the handle it is about\n * to await; only `ready` says the socket is bound (and, after a fallback, that\n * `port` is final).\n *\n * The token resolves **raw and unverified**. The listener\n    // has no nonce of its own to compare it against, so it ignores whatever\n    // comes back; the token is verified against the backend below instead.\n    const next = `http://${loopbackHost}:${callback.port}/auth/callback?state=${randomBytes(16).toString(\"hex\")}`;\n    const url = `${authBaseUrl}/cli/oauth?provider=${provider}&next=${encodeURIComponent(next)}`;\n\n    if (openBrowser(url)) {\n      console.log(`Opening ${providerLabel(provider)} sign-in in your browser...`);\n      console.log(`Nothing opened? Open this link to sign in:\\n  ${url}`);\n    }\n    console.log(`Waiting for the browser to come back on port ${callback.port}...`);\n\n    const sessionToken = await callback.token;\n    const user = await resolveUser(sessionToken);\n    saveSession(sessionToken, user);\n    console.log(`Signed in as ${user.email}`);\n    return { kind: \"signed_in\", method: provider, userId: user.id };\n  } finally {\n    callback.close();\n  }\n}\n\nexport type { AuthUser, StoredSession };\n\nexport function authConfigured(): boolean {\n  return Boolean(configuredAuthBaseUrl());\n}\n\nexport function storedSession(): StoredSession | null {\n  return readSession();\n}\n\nexport function storedUserId(): string | null {\n  return readSession()?.user.id ?? null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Validates a persisted session against the backend, dropping it if it no\n * longer resolves. When sign-in finishes, the\nWorker's `/cli/handoff` returns the browser to the waiting listener with the\nsession attached, and the terminal saves it exactly as a password sign-in would.\nOn macOS a default browser of Arc or Dia gets a real tab via AppleScript rather\nthan a plain `open`, because Little Arc drops the redirect chain the handoff\ndepends on.
